   - Further analysis shows that a set of nodes (B, C, J) interrupts all combinations effectively

- S is connected to E, B, and F.
- O is connected to N, C, H, and G.

1. S -> E -> B -> R -> J -> H -> O
2. S -> E -> B -> J -> H -> O
3. S -> E -> B -> C -> H -> O
4. S -> E -> B -> C -> N -> O

6. S -> E -> A -> N -> O
7. S -> F -> T -> R -> J -> H -> O
8. S -> F -> T -> R -> J -> Q -> I -> G -> O

Now let's identify nodes whose removal will disconnect these paths:
- Removing E eliminates paths 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, and 6.
- Removing R eliminates paths 1, 7.
- Removing J eliminates paths 1, 2, 7.

Next, finding the minimum set:
- If we remove E, we must also remove either R or J to ensure all paths are broken. This results in a minimum set size of 2.

Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [E, R]

This ensures that all connecting paths between S and O are eliminated by removing the minimum number of nodes.

To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed so that the two black nodes, S and O, are completely disconnected, we need to analyze the paths between these nodes and find the smallest set of nodes whose removal will eliminate all paths.

1. **Identify the black nodes**: S and O.
2. **Trace the paths between S and O**:
   - S → B → T → O
   - S → B → C → T → O
   - S → B → N → T → O
   - S → B → C → G → O
   - S → E → B → C → T → O
   - S → E → B → N → T → O
   - S → E → B → C → G → O
   - S → E → B → T → O

3. **Identify the nodes that, if removed, will disconnect the paths**:
   - Removing node B will disconnect S and O because B is a common node in all paths.
   - Removing node T will disconnect S and O because T is a common node in multiple paths.
   - Removing node C will disconnect S and O because C is a common node in multiple paths.

   However, removing any one of these nodes (B, T, or C) will not be sufficient because there are still alternative paths. We need to find the minimum set of nodes to remove.

4. **Check combinations of nodes**:
   - Removing nodes B and T will disconnect S and O because all paths involve either B or T.
   - Removing nodes B and C will also disconnect S and O because all paths involve either B or C.
   - Removing nodes T and C will also disconnect S and O because all paths involve either T or C.

Since removing either B and T, or B and C, or T and C will achieve the disconnection, the minimum number of nodes to remove is 2.

**Output Format**:
```
Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [B, T]
```
(Note: Any combination of [B, T], [B, C], or [T, C] is acceptable as the cut set.)
